Advancing hardware security with open-source Integrated HSM technology

📅 Oct 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At this year’s <a href="https://aka.ms/OCP2026" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">OCP Global Summit</a>, Microsoft announced Project Tessera, the evolution of the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/enforcing-trust-and-transparency-open-sourcing-the-azure-integrated-hsm/" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">Azure Integrated Hardware Security Module (HSM),</a> into a fully open-source initiative developed in collaboration with Marvell®, Oracle, and <a href="https://www.opencompute.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Open Compute Project (OCP)</a>. Project Tessera advances our broader commitment to open, industrywide standards for platform security.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The motivation behind Project Tessera is straightforward: the entire industry benefits when we collaborate and advance hardware security together.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Project Tessera carries forward the workload directly attached to the HSM architecture introduced by Azure Integrated HSM, <a href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">making the architecture, specifications, protocols, drivers, software, firmware, and reproducible build environments open source</a> for the broader community. Over time, the initiative will advance toward a fully open-source silicon implementation, while making hardware broadly available, enabling organizations across the ecosystem to adopt, integrate, and contribute to the platform.</p> <div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex"> <div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Explore the Tessera Integrated HSM GitHub</a></div> </div> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">This approach builds on the model Microsoft established with other industry collaborators on <a href="https://caliptra.io/" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">Caliptra</a>, a fully open-source <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/enforcing-trust-and-transparency-open-sourcing-the-azure-integrated-hsm/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">silicon root-of-trust that has since been broadly adopted</a> across the hardware ecosystem. Through Project Tessera, industry leaders including Microsoft, Marvell®, Oracle, and the Open Compute Project will apply that same model of transparency and collaboration to advance hardware key protection with the industry.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Image reads, " /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button></figure> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The cybersecurity landscape is rapidly changing, and protecting keys and credentials has never been more important. We are now facing two generational transformations that are fundamentally reshaping how we view the cybersecurity landscape:</p> <ol class="wp-block-list" start="1"> <li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>AI is increasing the speed and scale of cyberattacks</strong><br /><br />AI-orchestrated attacks are increasing the scale, speed, and sophistication of attacks, giving adversaries new ways to operate and compressing the time between vulnerability discovery and exploitation. These capabilities amplify risks such as credential theft, key exfiltration, and signing abuse.<br /><br /><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/security/fundamentals/key-management" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Keys and credentials are high-value control assets</a>. When compromised, they can enable attackers to impersonate trusted entities, bypass security controls, authorize malicious operations, expose sensitive data, and undermine software security defenses. Protecting these assets requires infrastructure with layered, hardened protections capable of withstanding sustained attacks across multiple concurrent attack vectors.</li> </ol> <ol class="wp-block-list" start="2"> <li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Quantum computing is changing the cryptographic risk horizon</strong><br /><br />Quantum computing promises transformative advances, but it also presents a risk to many of today’s cryptographic algorithms. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/project/post-quantum-cryptography/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">A sufficiently powerful quantum computer could break widely used public-key cryptography</a>, undermining digital signatures, authentication, and identity verification.<br /><br />Post-quantum cryptography was once viewed as an important but distant technology shift. Advances in quantum computing have changed that perspective as organizations recognize the urgency, scale, and complexity of the transition. <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/category/post-quantum-crypto-tech/blog/post-quantum-crypto-tech-blog" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">HSM-backed signing infrastructure will be among the first critical systems to migrate</a>, requiring HSMs that support post-quantum cryptography and are interoperable with classical HSMs to support migration.</li> </ol> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">These emerging threats motivated the development of <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/security/fundamentals/azure-integrated-hardware-security-module-overview" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Azure Integrated HSM</a>, which was designed to meet rigorous security standards for hardware-based key protection, while also accelerating Microsoft&#8217;s transition to post-quantum cryptography through scale deployments of quantum resilient integrated HSMs.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://github.com/microsoft/AziHSM-Guest" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Azure Integrated HSM</a> introduced a workload-attached HSM that performs cryptographic operations closer to the workload while keys remain continuously protected within the HSM security boundary. This architecture strengthens key protection, improves cryptographic performance, offers crypto-agility, and provides a simpler operational model.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Tessera is an evolution of Azure’s first integrated HSM model, incorporating insights gained from deploying Azure Integrated HSM at scale, and advances the technology through collaboration in the Open Compute Project, with the goal of broadly addressing the challenges of key and credential management in the era of AI and quantum computing.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="eliminating-key-management-anti-patterns"><strong>Eliminating key management anti-patterns</strong></h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">In central HSM deployment models, HSM infrastructure is separate from compute resources, requiring cryptographic operations to traverse the network. The resulting latency and operational dependencies can lead enterprises to adopt workarounds that improve performance or availability but unintentionally weaken key protections. Some of these common key-management anti-patterns include:</p> <ul class="wp-block-list"> <li class="wp-block-list-item">HSM keys may act as certificate authorities and endorse externally generated keys. These keys can then serve as trusted delegates without receiving the same hardware protection. </li> </ul> <ul class="wp-block-list"> <li class="wp-block-list-item">HSM generated keys may be created as extractable and exported from the HSM in wrapped form. When used outside of the HSM the keys may not receive assurance levels equivalent to a FIPS 140-3 Level 3 HSM and become more susceptible to exfiltration or misuse. </li> </ul> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Other anti-patterns include caching keys outside of HSMs for resilience, using software-held keys to reduce network latency to remote HSMs, sharing keys and security domains to simplify governance, and relying on proprietary protocols for interoperability. These practices intend to prioritize operational flexibility but can make key protection, policy enforcement, and auditable provenance less consistent across environments, undermining the security value HSMs are designed to provide. </p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The goal of Project Tessera is to advance the integrated HSM model to address these challenges in the industry, without compromising on security. The project will deliver Tessera Integrated HSM, a fully open-source integrated HSM designed to consistently enforce FIPS 140-3 Level 3 key protection throughout the key’s lifecycle.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">By combining strong hardware-rooted protection with an open-source implementation and open protocols, Tessera Integrated HSM creates a path toward greater transparency, interoperability, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/protecting-azure-infrastructure-from-silicon-to-systems/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">innovation across the HSM ecosystem</a>. The native support for quantum-resilient cryptography will help ease the industry’s transition to post-quantum cryptography.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Diagram showing a Tessera Integrated HSM protecting cryptographic keys in hardware while a virtual machine accesses them locally." class="wp-image-54513 webp-format" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/image-10.webp" /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 1: Tessera Integrated HSM</figcaption></figure> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="demonstrating-an-open-interoperable-hsm-ecosystem"><strong>Demonstrating an open, interoperable HSM ecosystem</strong> </h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today at the Open Compute Project Global Summit, Microsoft and Marvell® demonstrated the Tessera Integrated HSM transfer protocols establishing HSM partition cloning and resiliency rings across a Tessera Integrated HSM and a Marvell® LiquidSecurity® 2 HSM. The demonstration showed how two heterogeneous HSMs can operate in a resilient and unified security domain. Non-extractable keys within the security domain always remain protected within the HSMs and are available only to the authorized HSM partitions participating in the security domain and remain under the sole control of the partition owner.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Tessera Integrated HSM protocols use post-quantum cryptography to protect communication between HSMs. This enables authorized workloads with a Tessera Integrated HSM to use both classical cryptography and post-quantum cryptographic services while their keys remain secured within the HSM boundary and can only be authorized for use by the key owner.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Diagram showing Project Tessera protocols enabling HSM partition cloning between Tessera Integrated HSM and Marvell LiquidSecurity 2." class="wp-image-54514 webp-format" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/image-11.webp" /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 2: Partition cloning example</figcaption></figure> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Project Tessera specifications, protocols, software libraries, drivers, firmware, and hardware are available through the <a href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Tessera Integrated HSM GitHub</a>. Going forward, this repository will add more open-source silicon, new hardware designs, and enhancements developed with the community.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="expanding-project-tessera-through-confidential-computing"><strong>Expanding Project Tessera through confidential computing</strong></h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The early collaborators working on Project Tessera within the OCP community are exploring how the Tessera Integrated HSM key protection architecture can extend beyond systems with dedicated integrated HSM hardware. As part of this work, the project is making a minimal, unikernel-based Tessera firmware stack open source, designed to run within hardware-isolated <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/confidential-computing/overview" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">confidential computing environments</a>.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">This implementation uses the same firmware stack as the hardware integrated HSM, extending a common security architecture across HSMs and confidential computing environments. In this model, cryptographic keys are protected within a confidential computing container, which provides cryptographic services to securely bound workloads, including worker containers and virtual machines. The initial implementation leverages AMD SEV-SNP and integrates with open-source virtualization technologies such as <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/OpenVMM" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">OpenVMM</a>, with the Open Compute community working to extend support to other confidential computing architectures.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The complementary deployment models in Project Tessera extend strong key protection across systems with different hardware capabilities and assurance requirements. For platforms without dedicated integrated HSM hardware, confidential computing provides broadly deployable, hardware-isolated key protection with a minimal trusted computing base.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Project Tessera provides the highest level of hardware assurance, protecting keys within a dedicated, tamper-resistant security boundary designed to meet demanding certification requirements, including FIPS 140-3 Level 3 and Common Criteria. </p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Diagram comparing Project Tessera protection profiles across virtualization, confidential computing, and Integrated HSM deployments." class="wp-image-54598 webp-format" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Project-Tessera_Protection-Profiles-1024x576.webp" /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 3: Protection profiles for assurance levels</figcaption></figure> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">The deployment models share the same Project Tessera firmware and programming model, giving organizations a consistent foundation for cryptographic security across their infrastructure while providing a natural path to integrated HSM and higher levels of assurance in newer systems.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these models make strong key protection accessible across a broad range of systems with different security assurance needs, all built on a common, open architecture. As the Open Compute Project community continues to advance Tessera Integrated HSM, we see an exciting opportunity for broader industry collaboration to strengthen key protection, expand secure key management, and accelerate innovation across the ecosystem.</p> <div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex"> <div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Explore the Tessera Integrated HSM GitHub</a></div> </div> <hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" /> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">For more information, visit the Microsoft booth at the Open Compute Project Global Summit for live demonstrations, <a href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">explore Tessera Integrated HSM implementation on GitHub</a>, and read the Microsoft Infrastructure Community blog for additional hardware security announcements: <a Security Updates</a>.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Check out Microsoft’s presence at OCP Summit this year: <a href="http://aka.ms/OCP2026" rel="noopener noreferrer" Infrastructure for the frontier era: Scaling intelligence through open industry collaboration

📅 Oct 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At Microsoft, our vision is to build a <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/building-the-frontier-firm-with-microsoft-azure-the-business-case-for-cloud-and-ai-modernization/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Frontier Firm</a>: one where people and technology work together, combining human expertise, context, and judgment with technology that can extend intelligence throughout everyday work. The potential is significant: helping people create, solve complex problems, and make progress on some of society’s most consequential challenges.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/explore/global-infrastructure/silicon-systems" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">But realizing that potential requires frontier infrastructure</a>. The next phase of Super Intelligence needs a unified infrastructure engine that delivers more intelligence with greater efficiency, making AI more available, reliable, and sustainable across science, healthcare, education, and the economy. This is the work of an entire ecosystem, and precisely the vision we have advanced for years through our industry collaboration within the Open Compute Project Foundation.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="designing-silicon-in-the-loop-with-real-workloads">Designing silicon in the loop with real workloads</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">As we enter the next phase of Super Intelligence, inference is becoming the dominant workload and that’s where efficiency, latency, and cost really matter. <a href="http://aka.ms/Maia300" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Azure Maia 300</a>, our next generation AI accelerator from our Maia family, advances the inference efficiency frontier. Maia 300 is a complete system designed for the new types of AI workloads emerging today: long-context reasoning, latency-sensitive conversational experiences, and sustained, multi-step agentic tasks. By improving efficiency across the system, we expect Maia 300 to deliver 40% more tokens per watt compared to leading GPU-based systems. And in a full 96-accelerator configuration, it delivers up to seven times the rack-scale token throughput of Maia 200.</p> <div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex"> <div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="http://aka.ms/Maia300" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">Learn more about Maia 300</a></div> </div> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/01/26/maia-200-the-ai-accelerator-built-for-inference/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Maia</a> reflects our system co-design approach. It is designed alongside Microsoft infrastructure and production workloads, bringing models, application frameworks, kernels, communication libraries, systems software, and custom silicon into one optimization loop. Maia 200 is deploying across the United States, Europe and Australia and delivering up to 1.7x more tokens per watt for built-in AI capabilities in Microsoft Teams, <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/copilot" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Copilot</a> and Cowork experiences compared with leading GPU infrastructure.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Azure Maia 200 systems installed in a Microsoft datacenter." class="wp-image-54582 webp-format" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Phoenix-Data-Center-Shoot-2026_Stills-2-1-scaled.webp" /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Azure Maia 200 systems in a datacenter</figcaption></figure> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI is not only about accelerators. Agentic workloads create entirely new demands on CPUs. Every agent interaction generates orchestration, data retrieval, memory management, and service calls that must occur quickly and efficiently. That is why we designed Cobalt 200 as an agent-native CPU. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-cobalt-200-vms-deliver-50-performance-improvement-fully-optimized-for-modern-agentic-ai-workloads/">Cobalt 200</a> delivers more than 50% performance improvement over Cobalt 100 and reduces agent call latency by 33%, while providing 23% higher throughput on agentic workloads.</p> <figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img alt="Rows of Azure Cobalt 200 server systems installed in a Microsoft datacenter." class="wp-image-54583 webp-format" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Azure-Hardware-Media-Selects_Blurred-16-scaled.webp" /><button class="lightbox-trigger" type="button"> <svg fill="none" height="12" viewBox="0 0 12 12" width="12" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"> <path d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" fill="#fff"> </svg> </button><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Azure Cobalt 200 systems in a datacenter</figcaption></figure> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/explore/global-infrastructure/silicon-systems" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Maia and Cobalt are designed in the loop</a> with our largest enterprise workloads. That gives Microsoft a unique advantage in tuning performance, reliability, and cost to exactly what customers need in production.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are applying the same principle to storage and security. The <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azureinfrastructureblog/enhancing-infrastructure-efficiency-with-azure-boost-dpu/4298901">Azure Boost Data Processing Unit</a> moves compression, networking transactions, and security functions away from the CPU onto purpose-built silicon, enabling us to deliver four times more performance while using significantly less power.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">And <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/enforcing-trust-and-transparency-open-sourcing-the-azure-integrated-hsm/">Azure Integrated HSM</a> makes security part of the hardware foundation, with our in-house security chip in all new datacenter servers. It brings cryptographic operations closer to the workload while keeping keys protected inside the hardware security boundary—not exposed in application memory.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="building-an-open-resilient-ai-fabric">Building an open, resilient AI fabric</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/theme/future-ai-infrastructure/networking/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Compute creates intelligence</a> and networks make it usable at scale.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Working with industry partners, Microsoft developed <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azurehighperformancecomputingblog/building-resilient-networks-for-ai-supercomputers/4516919">Multipath Reliable Connection (MRC)</a>, rethinking Ethernet for AI. Its multi-plane architecture scales beyond 100,000 GPUs with two network tiers, reducing power, complexity, failure points, and cost. By intelligently distributing traffic across multiple paths and through packet spraying, MRC limits congestion and maintains reliable performance for frontier-scale training.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are also advancing open scale-up connectivity through Optical Compute Interconnect Multi-Source Agreement (<a href="https://oci-msa.org/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">OCI MSA</a>). OCI, the first optical specification for multi-rack scale-up systems, enables high-bandwidth, power-efficient, multi-vendor infrastructure. Moving forward, we will continue to work with the industry to expand participation in OCI MSA.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">In parallel, The <a href="https://www.opencompute.org/news/introducing-esun-advancing-ethernet-for-scale-up-ai-infrastructure-at-ocp/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Ethernet for Scale-Up Networking</a> (ESUN) workstream brings together more than 175 organizations to define requirements for Ethernet-based scale-up networking. The group has released specification version 1.0 and is developing version 1.1 for later this year. Read more about our <a href="https://aka.ms/rethinking-networking-ocp26" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">networking innovations with the open community</a>.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="rethinking-power-from-grid-to-silicon">Rethinking power from grid to silicon</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI infrastructure is also forcing a fundamental redesign of power delivery. Through OCP and the Open Systems for AI initiative, Microsoft is helping <a href="https://www.opencompute.org/news/powering-the-next-era-of-ai-how-google-microsoft-and-nvidia-are-standardizing-and-accelerating-the-industry-transition-to-lvdc/">advance the industry&#8217;s transition</a> toward 800 volt direct current (VDC) infrastructure.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Mt. Diablo, Microsoft&#8217;s disaggregated high-voltage datacenter (DC) sidecar architecture, separates power conversion from the compute rack so each can scale independently. It includes a modular design supporting approximately 100 kilowatts (kW) to beyond one megawatt (MW), with redundant feeds, telemetry and optional energy storage. Microsoft, together with OCP partners, contributed Mt. Diablo specifications v0.7 and v1.0 to OCP.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft and its partners are advancing solid-state transformer-based architectures to make power conversion more efficient, responsive, and scalable for next-generation 800 VDC datacenters. Through OCP’s Clean Backup Power initiative, we are developing open requirements for reliable backup systems that reduce reliance on diesel. In parallel, Microsoft collaborated with partners to release a common grid-interface specification to align utilities, operators, and equipment vendors as hyperscale power architectures evolve. Read more about <a href="https://aka.ms/azure-grid-to-chip-power" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">Microsoft’s approach to 800VDC transition and scaling power infrastructure sustainably</a>.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="cooling-as-a-first-class-design-consideration">Cooling as a first-class design consideration</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft approaches cooling as a datacenter-wide challenge, driving innovation from DC facility design to rack-level liquid cooling technologies.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our third-generation Azure Heat Exchanger Unit, Mt. Atlas, nearly doubles cooling capacity compared to the previous generation while maintaining the same operational footprint. We are also contributing public reference architecture through OCP to help accelerate industry adoption.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">At the datacenter level, Microsoft is advancing a <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/AzureArchitectureBlog/flexible-cooling-for-ai-growth-how-zonal-architecture-supports-diverse-hardware-/4514042">zonal cooling</a> strategy in its next-generation AI datacenters to handle the growing mix of liquid-cooled accelerators and traditional air-cooled systems<strong>.</strong> By tailoring cooling to the specific needs of each workload, and introducing multiple independent water loops, each supplying coolant at different temperatures, we aim to increase efficiency and performance while supporting Microsoft’s energy, carbon, and water reduction goals.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="open-hardware-security-innovation">Open hardware security innovation</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/protecting-azure-infrastructure-from-silicon-to-systems/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Microsoft believes strong cloud security is built through openness</a>, transparency, and industry collaboration. This principle is reflected in <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/confidential-computing/overview" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">our founding role in the Confidential Computing Consortium</a>, our work to establish Caliptra as an open-source silicon root of trust, and our contributions to the OCP S.A.F.E. security conformance framework. As AI reshapes the cybersecurity landscape and advances in quantum computing introduce new cryptographic risks, protecting the keys and credentials at the foundation of digital trust has never been more important.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">To address these challenges, and building on our commitment to security transparency and industry collaboration, Microsoft, Marvell, and Oracle are introducing <a href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Tessera Integrated HSM</a>, an open-source initiative for integrated hardware security modules. Tessera Integrated HSM carries forward the workload direct attached HSM architecture introduced by Azure Integrated HSM, <a href="https://github.com/TesseraHSM" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">open-sourcing</a> its architecture, specifications, protocols, drivers, software, firmware, and silicon to the broader community. <a href="https://aka.ms/project-tessera-ocp" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Learn more about Tessera Integrated HSM</a>.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="fleet-resiliency-at-scale">Fleet resiliency at scale</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">As AI infrastructure scales, fleet reliability is essential to protecting customer workloads, capacity, and service quality. Through OCP, Microsoft has published common RAS, debugging, telemetry, and diagnostics specifications, while contributing work on hardware fault management, CPU and GPU management, and fleet memory fault management. We are using years of fleet data and using AI to validate, predict, and drive fleet actions aimed at preventing faults, pinpointing issues, and recovering more autonomously. Learn more about how <a href="http://aka.ms/ocp26-ras-api" rel="noreferrer noopener" target="_blank">Microsoft is advancing fleet reliability through industry collaboration</a>.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="advancing-sustainability-across-the-infrastructure-stack">Advancing sustainability across the infrastructure stack</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Scaling AI responsibly means considering the full datacenter lifecycle to help reach our 2030 commitments to be carbon negative, water positive, and zero waste. As a company, we are committed to incorporating sustainability into how we design, build, and operate our datacenters extending to the communities where we build them. Through OCP, Microsoft is collaborating with partners on low-embodied-carbon steel and concrete—with pathways to reduce concrete emissions by more than 50% per cubic yard.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are advancing waste-heat reuse, and helping develop Cloud Services Product Category Rules for consistent, auditable lifecycle-carbon reporting. Inside our datacenters, closed-loop liquid cooling, intelligent power management, and renewable-energy agreements help reduce resource use, while Circular Centers reuse or recycle more than 90% of servers and components. Beyond the datacenter, we are investing in local skills, partnerships, and infrastructure so the communities hosting this growth can grow with us. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/corporate-responsibility/topics/sustainability/report/" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read Microsoft’s 2026 Environmental Sustainability report</a>.</p> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="building-the-frontier-together">Building the frontier together</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our vision is to help every organization become a Frontier Firm, uniting human expertise with AI in a continuous learning loop that turns knowledge and ingenuity into scalable intelligence. Realizing that vision requires full-stack co-design: silicon, compute, memory, networking, power, cooling, models, and applications working as one secure, reliable, efficient, and sustainable platform while giving customers choice without passing complexity on to them.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Such a future cannot be realized by a single organization. Through OCP, we are advancing open interfaces, sharing lessons from operating infrastructure at global scale, and collaborating across the industry so innovation moves faster, and the ecosystem stops solving the same problems repeatedly. Together, we can transform frontier innovation into infrastructure that makes intelligence accessible and affordable for every organization, in every industry, and across every region of the world.</p> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">At OCP Global Summit 2026, we invite the community to build that future with us.</p> <div class="is-style-inline wp-block-bloginabox-theme-promotional"> <div class="promotional promotional--has-media promotional--media-right"> <div class="promotional__wrapper"> <div class="promotional__content-wrapper"> <div class="promotional__content"> <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="build-for-what-s-next">Build for what&#8217;s next</h2> <p class="wp-block-paragraph">Learn more about Microsoft&#8217;s systems approach to infrastructure.</p> <div class="wp-block-buttons is-layout-flex wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex"> <div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="http://aka.ms/silicontosystems" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Start here</a></div> </div> </div> </div> <div class="promotional__media-wrapper"> <div class="promotional__media"> <img alt="People working in office." class="attachment-full size-full" height="1707" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/CLO20b_Madeleine_office_002-scaled.jpg" width="2560" /> </div> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/infrastructure-for-the-frontier-era-scaling-intelligence-through-open-industry-collaboration/">Infrastructure for the frontier era: Scaling intelligence through open industry collaboration</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>

AWS Weekly Roundup: Prime Day 2026 by the numbers, Claude Haiku 5.5, Amazon Nova 2.5 Sonic, and more (October 13, 2026)

📅 Oct 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p>Some numbers are hard to picture until you sit with them for a moment. Last week we published the Prime Day 2026 metrics, and the one that stuck with me is 2.3 quadrillion: the daily requests <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/elasticache/">Amazon ElastiCache</a> peaked at during the event. Prime Day ran back in June, so this post always lands as a retrospective, and that gap is part of what makes it worth reading. By the time the numbers reach you, the only evidence that any of it happened is that nothing went wrong.</p> <p>The rest of the week pulled in a different direction: smaller, cheaper, and more constrained. Claude Haiku 5.5 arrived on <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/">Amazon Bedrock</a> at roughly 75 percent less than Haiku 4.5, Amazon Nova 2.5 Sonic made voice agents quicker on their feet, and Strands Box showed up to put real boundaries around what an agent is allowed to do. Now, let’s get into this week’s AWS news…</p> <p><span id="more-105986"></span></p> <p><strong>Headlines</strong><br /> <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/all-the-numbers-amazon-prime-day-2026-powered-by-aws/">All the numbers: Amazon Prime Day 2026 powered by AWS</a> is the latest entry in a tradition that goes back to 2016, and the scale is still difficult to hold in your head. Over the four days of Prime Day, <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/">Amazon DynamoDB</a> processed more than 59 trillion requests while peaking at 192 million requests per second, and kept single-digit millisecond responses throughout. <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/">AWS Lambda</a> handled over 2.3 trillion invocations per day. <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ebs/">Amazon EBS</a> peaked above 24.8 trillion I/O operations, moving more than an exabyte of data daily. <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ecs/">Amazon ECS</a> launched an average of 158.3 million tasks per day on <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/fargate/">AWS Fargate</a>, a 47.7 percent jump over last year, and <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/graviton/">AWS Graviton</a> powered up to 49 percent of the <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/">Amazon EC2</a> compute that Amazon.com used.</p> <p>The number I keep coming back to, though, isn’t a throughput record. The team ran more than 44,000 <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/fis/">AWS Fault Injection Service</a> experiments ahead of the event, more than six times what they ran in 2025. Those are deliberate failures injected on purpose, months in advance, so that the trillions of requests above turn into an ordinary shopping afternoon. If you’re preparing for your own peak moment, <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/premiumsupport/aws-countdown/">AWS Countdown Premium</a> is built for exactly that kind of planning.</p> <p><strong>Last week’s launches</strong><br /> Here are some launches and updates from this past week that caught my attention:</p> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-claude-haiku-5-5-on-aws/">Claude Haiku 5.5 is now available on AWS</a> – You can now use the fastest model in the Claude 5.5 family on Amazon Bedrock and Claude Platform on AWS, at around 75 percent less than Claude Haiku 4.5 for most tasks. It’s the first Haiku with effort controls, so you can tune cost against intelligence per task instead of fixing one setting for a whole workload. It pairs naturally with Claude Opus 5.5: Opus plans and makes the judgment calls, and because Haiku is fast and cheap, you can run many Haiku subagents in parallel for routing, classification, and small code changes across many files.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/amazon-nova-2.5-sonic/">Amazon Nova 2.5 Sonic for voice agents</a> – You can now build real-time voice agents on a speech-to-speech <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/nova/">Amazon Nova</a> model with better reasoning, instruction following, and tool-calling accuracy, plus lower latency. That combination matters for multi-step work: a support agent can look up an order, check return eligibility, and start the return inside one conversation. It keeps expressive voices in seven languages, controllable turn-taking, and a 256K context window, and pairs with Strands Bidi Agents, <a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/bidi-agents-now-ga/">now generally available</a>, to get a production voice agent going in a few lines of code.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/guardduty-rds-data-exfiltration/">Amazon GuardDuty RDS Protection now detects data exfiltration and destruction</a> – <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/guardduty/">Amazon GuardDuty</a> now looks past login anomalies to the queries themselves on <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/rds/aurora/">Amazon Aurora</a> PostgreSQL and <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/rds/">Amazon RDS</a> for PostgreSQL. RDS Protection for data activity builds behavioral baselines and uses ML-based anomaly detection plus threat intelligence to flag unusual data access, with no agents, extra infrastructure, or database configuration changes. This closes a real gap: once an attacker has valid credentials from phishing or a misconfiguration, their queries can look indistinguishable from your application’s.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/aws-Lambda-supports-oauth-kafka-esm/">AWS Lambda supports OAuth for self-managed Apache Kafka event sources</a> – You can now authenticate Lambda Kafka consumers through your enterprise identity provider, such as Amazon Cognito or Okta. Event source mappings previously supported only SASL/PLAIN, SASL/SCRAM, and mTLS, which left teams in regulated industries unable to use Kafka ESM at all. Now you get the automatic scaling, batching, error handling, and event filtering of Kafka ESM with the same identity governance you apply everywhere else. It works with self-hosted clusters and managed offerings including Confluent Cloud, Aiven, and Redpanda.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/redshift-iceberg-materialized-views">Amazon Redshift adds Apache Iceberg materialized views</a> – You can now pre-compute expensive joins and aggregations once in <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/redshift/">Amazon Redshift</a> and store the result as an Iceberg table in <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/">Amazon S3</a>, registered in the <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/glue/">AWS Glue</a> Data Catalog. You create them with familiar SQL, and the results are immediately queryable by any Iceberg-compatible engine, including <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/athena/">Amazon Athena</a>, Apache Spark, Trino, and Snowflake. Redshift keeps them current by recomputing only what changed. There’s a <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/big-data/materialize-once-query-anywhere-introducing-iceberg-materialized-views-in-amazon-redshift/">good deep dive on the AWS Big Data blog</a> if you want the details.</li> </ul> <p>For a full list of AWS announcements, be sure to keep an eye on the <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/new/">What’s New with AWS</a> page.</p> <p><strong>Other AWS news</strong><br /> Here are some additional posts and resources that you might find interesting:</p> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/opensource/introducing-strands-box-ai-agent-sandboxes-powered-by-dogwood/">Introducing Strands Box: AI agent sandboxes powered by Dogwood</a> – As more of us let agents run shell commands and call APIs without approving every step, isolation alone stops being enough. <a href="https://github.com/strands-agents/box">Strands Box</a> is an open source sandbox (Apache 2.0, developer preview) that combines OS-level containment with fine-grained policies written in Dogwood, so permissions can depend on what the agent has already done. You can say “let the agent post to the incident channel, but no more than three times every 10 minutes” and have that enforced from outside the agent’s process. It also keeps real credentials out of the agent’s environment by attaching them at an egress gateway.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/security/aws-continuum-sets-a-new-standard-in-autonomous-code-security/">AWS Continuum sets a new standard in autonomous code security</a> – Related to that theme, AWS Continuum for Penetration Testing <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2026/10/aws-continuum-penetration-testing/">now integrates into your CI/CD pipeline</a> in public preview, turning penetration testing into a deploy-time event instead of something that happens off to the side. Developers get findings with severity, affected endpoints, and remediation guidance right in the pipeline output, and the pipeline re-tests to verify fixes.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-postman-runs-agent-mode-for-40-million-developers-on-amazon-bedrock/">How Postman runs Agent Mode for 40 million developers on Amazon Bedrock</a> – A look at the architecture behind a feature serving a very large developer audience. Worth reading if you’re moving an agentic feature from a demo to something that has to hold up under real traffic.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/icymi-what-landed-for-ai-builders-in-september-2026/">ICYMI: What landed for AI builders in September 2026</a> – If you lost track of a month that included a lot of model launches, this is a tidy catch-up in one place.</li> </ul> <p><strong>Upcoming AWS events</strong><br /> Check your calendar and sign up for upcoming AWS events:</p> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/events/reinvent/">AWS re:Invent</a> – re:Invent returns to Las Vegas from November 30 to December 4, and reserved seating is now open. The session catalog is live, so <a href="https://registration.awsevents.com/flow/awsevents/reinvent2026/reg">register</a> and claim your spots in chalk talks, workshops, and builders’ sessions before they fill up.</li> </ul> <ul> <li><a href="https://aws.amazon.com/events/community-day/">AWS Community Days</a> – Community-led conferences planned and delivered by community leaders, and this is a busy stretch. Upcoming stops include <a href="https://www.aws-aicd.com/">San Francisco</a> (October 13), <a href="https://awscommunitydaysoflo.com/">Miami</a> (October 14), <a href="https://awscommunitydayaus.com/">Brisbane</a> (October 16), <a href="https://www.awscommunityday.co.za/">Cape Town</a> (October 16), <a href="https://www.awscommunityday.mt/">Malta</a> (October 16), and <a href="https://acd.awsugmum.in/">Mumbai</a> (October 17). Here in Brazil, the <a href="https://awscommunityday.com.br/">community calendar</a> has more editions coming before the end of the year.</li> </ul> <p>Join the <a href="https://builder.aws.com/build/capabilities?trk=f11c83db-08fa-4852-aa10-20185beeaef6&amp;sc_channel=el">AWS Builder Center</a> to connect with builders, share solutions, and access content that supports your development. Browse <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/events/">here</a> for upcoming AWS-led in-person and virtual events and developer-focused events.</p> <p>That’s all for this week. Check back next Monday for another Weekly Roundup!</p> <p>– Daniel Abib</p> <p><em>This post is part of our Weekly Roundup series. Check back each week for a quick roundup of interesting news and announcements from AWS!</em></p>

Announcing AWS Lambda Web Functions

📅 Oct 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p>Today, we’re announcing <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/lambda-web-functions/">AWS Lambda Web Functions</a>, a new form factor that lets you run standard HTTP web applications on Lambda without rewriting your code into an event handler. You can take a standard port-listening server built with Express, Hono, Fastify, Next.js, or any Node.js framework, deploy it with a single command, and Lambda automatically handles scaling, multi-region distribution, and active CPU billing. No adapters, no infrastructure to manage, and no framework lock-in.</p> <p>With this launch, AWS Lambda now offers three distinct form factors, each built on the same Firecracker isolation technology and designed for a different programming model.</p> <p><strong>Lambda Event Functions</strong> are what you already know as Lambda functions. They use the event-driven, single-concurrent handler model with wall-clock billing. We’re introducing the “Event Functions” name to highlight that programming model: your function receives an event, processes it, and returns a response. The event-driven programming model is ideal for event processing, queue consumers, and invocations triggered by other AWS services.</p> <p><strong>Lambda Web Functions</strong> use an HTTP-native programming model with multi-concurrent execution and active CPU billing. Your code listens on a port and handles standard HTTP requests. That programming model is ideal for web applications, API backends, and interactive HTTP workloads where multiple concurrent requests and I/O-heavy processing are the norm.</p> <p><strong>Lambda MicroVMs</strong> provide isolated, stateful sandbox environments with customer-managed lifecycles for running untrusted or user-generated code.</p> <p>The three complement each other. An application might use Event Functions for asynchronous backend processing, Web Functions for the user-facing API and server-side rendering layer, and MicroVMs for interactive code execution features, all within the same AWS account and sharing the same operational model.</p> <p><span style="text-decoration: underline;"><strong>Why Web Functions<br /> </strong></span>Modern web applications need to serve global audiences with fast, consistent response times while giving developers the freedom to use standard frameworks and iterate quickly. Building this today typically requires assembling multiple infrastructure components, configuring multi-region deployments, setting up latency-based routing, and tuning billing models to avoid paying for I/O wait. Developers often find themselves adapting their application code to fit a specific compute model rather than deploying the HTTP server they already have.</p> <p>Lambda Web Functions removes this complexity. Each execution environment handles multiple requests simultaneously (up to 128 concurrent requests per environment), which means a burst of incoming traffic can share warm environments instead of mapping each request to a new one. Web Functions meter the CPU time your code uses, so time spent waiting on a database query or an API call consumes little CPU. Memory is metered for as long as the execution environment exists. For I/O-heavy web workloads like server-side rendering, API backends, and AI inference endpoints streaming token responses, this can reduce compute costs.</p> <p><span style="text-decoration: underline;"><strong>Let’s try it out<br /> </strong></span>To get started, I wrote a simple Express.js server. This is the same code I run locally with <code>node index.js</code>, no Lambda-specific adapters or wrappers required.</p> <pre><code class="language-javascript">import express from 'express'; const app = express(); const [host, port] = process.env.AWS_LAMBDA_HTTP_ENDPOINT.split(':'); app.get('/', (req, res) =&gt; { res.json({ message: 'Hello from Lambda Web Functions!' }); }); app.listen(port, host, () =&gt; { console.log(`Server running on port ${port}`); }); </code></pre> <p>The only difference from running locally is the <code>AWS_LAMBDA_HTTP_ENDPOINT</code> environment variable, which Lambda sets to the host and port your server must listen on. To run locally, set it yourself, for example <code>AWS_LAMBDA_HTTP_ENDPOINT=127.0.0.1:3000 node index.js</code>.</p> <p>With my code ready, I deployed using the <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cli/">AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI)</a>:</p> <pre><code>aws lambda-web deploy --name my-web-app --code ./my-app </code></pre> <p>One command. No prior resource setup. The CLI bundled my source, created the Web Function, published its first revision, attached a Web Function endpoint, and returned a live HTTPS URL in under a minute.</p> <p>A revision is an immutable snapshot of the code I deployed. The endpoint is the HTTPS URL that routes traffic to a revision, or splits traffic across revisions. Those are the resources I use to iterate and to control how traffic moves to new code.</p> <p>I tested it immediately:</p> <pre><code>$ curl https://&lt;domain-id&gt;.&lt;shard-id&gt;.lambda-web.us-east-1.on.aws/ {"message":"Hello from Lambda Web Functions!"} </code></pre> <p>Next, I wanted my application to serve users in Europe and Asia with lower latency. I redeployed with multiple regions:</p> <pre><code>aws lambda-web deploy --name my-web-app --code ./my-app \ --regions us-east-1,eu-west-1,ap-northeast-1 </code></pre> <p>Lambda Web Functions replicated my code to all three regions and configured latency-based routing automatically. Each request now routes to the Region with the lowest latency for the caller, on a best-effort basis, through one global URL (<code>&lt;domain-id&gt;.lambda-web.global.on.aws</code>).</p> <p>When I updated my code and ran the same deploy command again, the CLI detected the existing function, uploaded the new revision, and pointed the endpoint at it. Total turnaround from code change to live: seconds.</p> <p>For production workflows that require a more gradual rollout, I can shift traffic between revisions with a single API call:</p> <pre><code>aws lambda-web update-web-function-endpoint \ --function-name my-web-app \ --endpoint-name dev \ --auto-deployment-mode Disabled \ --revision-weights '[{"revisionId": "&lt;current-revision-id&gt;", "weight": 90}, {"revisionId": "&lt;new-revision-id&gt;", "weight": 10}]' </code></pre> <p>This sends 90% of endpoint traffic to the current revision and 10% to the new one, giving me a canary deployment without additional tooling.</p> <p><span style="text-decoration: underline;"><strong>Better together with Amazon CloudFront<br /> </strong></span>For applications that combine static and dynamic content, Web Functions works well alongside <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudfront/">Amazon CloudFront</a>. You can select a Web Function endpoint as the origin for your CloudFront distribution. Static assets are served from the cache while dynamic requests route to your Web Function, giving you CDN performance for static content with serverless compute for the dynamic parts.</p> <p>Web Functions improves the private-origin experience with CloudFront.&nbsp;With this launch, you attach CloudFront <a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/private-content-restricting-access-to-origin.html">origin access control (OAC)</a> to a Web Function endpoint instead. CloudFront signs requests to the origin, and the endpoint allows only your distribution to invoke it. You get the same private-origin pattern CloudFront already provides for <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/">Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3)</a>, without exposing the endpoint or adding custom authorization to your app.</p> <p><span style="text-decoration: underline;"><strong>Now available<br /> </strong></span>AWS Lambda Web Functions is available today in US East (N. Virginia), US East (Ohio), US West (Oregon), Asia Pacific (Tokyo), and Europe (Ireland). Web Functions run Node.js 24 (nodejs24.x) on Arm64 at launch and support Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) connectivity, allowing your function to access resources that exist within a VPC.</p> <p>To get started, deploy your first Web Function in under a minute:</p> <pre><code>aws lambda-web deploy --name hello-world --hello-world </code></pre> <p>For pricing details, visit the <a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/pricing/">AWS Lambda pricing page</a>. To learn more, see the <a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/web-functions.html">Lambda Web Functions documentation</a>.</p>

FBI Arrests Executive at Ransomware Negotiation Firm

📅 Oct 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p>Agents with the <strong>Federal Bureau of Investigation</strong> (FBI) on Thursday arrested an executive at a Canadian cybersecurity firm in connection with an investigation into the <strong>ShinyHunters</strong> hacking group that recently relieved the FBI of sensitive data on thousands of agents, multiple sources tell KrebsOnSecurity.</p> <p>The New York Times <a href="https://www.nytimes.com/2026/10/09/us/politics/fbi-hack-shinyhunters-arrest.html" rel="noopener" target="_blank">reported today</a> that the FBI has arrested a Canadian man in Pennsylvania on suspicion of assisting ShinyHunters. The Times story did not identify the man, nor did <a href="https://x.com/FBIDirectorKash/status/2108566367011999780?s=46&amp;t=jxLTOF6G9gZnhmWqh93ZrA" rel="noopener" target="_blank">a statement</a> on Twitter/X about the arrest from <strong>FBI Director Kash Patel</strong>.</p> <p>One source close to the investigation told KrebsOnSecurity the Canadian person arrested this week was visiting Pennsylvania for a cyber insurance conference, and that the suspect&#8217;s company specialized in handling ransomware negotiations with cybercrime groups. Another shared that control over the ShinyHunters investigation has been centralized at an FBI field office in Texas.</p> <p>An online search reveals the <a href="https://netdiligence.com/conferences/cyber-risk-summit-philadelphia-2026/speakers" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Cyber Risk Summit</a> was held at the Loews Philadelphia Hotel between Oct. 5 and Oct. 7. The conference had several sponsors, but according to the summit&#8217;s website its <a href="https://netdiligence.com/conferences/cyber-risk-summit-philadelphia-2026/sponsors" rel="noopener" target="_blank">biggest sponsor</a> was a Canadian security company called <strong>Cypfer</strong>.</p> <p>According to LinkedIn, one of Cypfer&#8217;s &#8220;ex-founders&#8221; was <strong>Edward Dubrovsky</strong>, who is now associated with another Canadian security firm and sponsor called <strong>CyberSteward</strong>. In a post to LinkedIn approximately one month ago, Dubrovsky said he had plans to attend the Cyber Risk Summit with the rest of the CyberSteward team.</p> <p>[<strong>Update: Oct. 10, 9:58 a.m. ET:</strong> A spokesperson for Cypfer said Dubrovsky was not a founder or co-founder as his LinkedIn profile claims, but instead served as a managing director before resigning in December 2025].</p> <div class="wp-caption aligncenter" id="attachment_74445" style="width: 798px;"><img alt="" class="size-full wp-image-74445" height="495" src="https://krebsonsecurity.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/dubrovsky-li.png" width="788" /><p class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-74445">Edward Dubrovsky&#8217;s LinkedIn profile.</p></div> <p>&#8220;Looking forward to continuing conversations around strategy &amp; compliant driven coercive (ransomware, extortion) advisory, negotiations and settlement services that are global and truly agnostic,&#8221; Dubrovsky wrote.</p> <p>Federal court records show that on October 8, an Edward Dobrovsky (note the slight misspelling of the last name) was arrested in Pennsylvania on cyber extortion and conspiracy charges. Several of those documents &#8212; including the core complaint &#8212; are now sealed. But a handful of them were <a href="https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/74942095/united-states-v-dobrovsky/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">indexed at Courtlistener.com</a>, including a summary of the complaint, which charges the defendant with &#8220;conspiracy to threaten to impair the confidentiality of information with the intent to extort money,&#8221; and &#8220;interference with commerce by threats.&#8221;</p> <div class="wp-caption aligncenter" id="attachment_74446" style="width: 759px;"><img alt="" class=" wp-image-74446" height="597" src="https://krebsonsecurity.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/dubrovsky-courtlistener.png" width="749" /><p class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-74446">Image: Courtlistener.com</p></div> <p>The inmate locator at the <strong>U.S. Bureau of Prisons</strong> website reports that a 54-year-old Edward Dubrovsky is currently being held at a federal facility in Philadelphia. But <a href="https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/74942095/united-states-v-dobrovsky/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">the court records</a> indexed by CourtListener include a notice filed on October 9 that moved the case to the Eastern District of Texas, which sources say is now the epicenter of the FBI&#8217;s ShinyHunters investigation. The FBI declined to comment for this story.<span id="more-74440"></span></p> <p>Dubrovsky&#8217;s <a href="https://www.linkedin.com/in/edubrovs/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">LinkedIn profile</a> states he is the author of <em>Cyber Extortion Strategic Response</em>, a 252-page book that promises to &#8220;take readers beyond the ransom note and into the decisions that determine how an organization responds, recovers, and protects what matters.&#8221;</p> <div class="wp-caption aligncenter" id="attachment_74441" style="width: 521px;"><img alt="" class="size-full wp-image-74441" height="745" src="https://krebsonsecurity.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/dubrovskybook.png" width="511" /><p class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-74441">Edward Dubrovsky&#8217;s book, which centers on the intricacies of ransomware negotiations.</p></div> <p>&#8220;At the heart of the book is a critical distinction: communicating with a criminal is not the same as negotiating a payment, and negotiating is not a commitment to pay,&#8221; reads an excerpt from the book&#8217;s listing on Amazon. &#8220;Engagement can serve other objectives, including testing claims, gathering information, creating time, and preserving options while the organization evaluates its next move.&#8221;</p> <p>Mr. Dubrovsky could not be immediately reached for comment. KrebsOnSecurity also sought comment from other executives at CyberSteward, and will update this post in the event they respond. The available court records in Dubrovsky&#8217;s case show that he does not currently have an attorney and has yet to be appointed a public defender by the courts.</p> <p>ShinyHunters typically uses phishing and stolen credentials to siphon data from corporate accounts at software-as-a-service companies, and then threatens to publish the stolen data online unless a ransom demand is paid. According to the FBI, the group has extorted more than $70 million from victims so far this year.</p> <p>Sources tell KrebsOnSecurity the FBI has been poring over devices that were seized last month when the Dutch police <a href="https://krebsonsecurity.com/2026/09/dutch-police-arrest-reformed-hacker-in-shiny-hunters-investigation/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">arrested the convicted cybercriminal Pepijn van der Stap</a> in connection with the ShinyHunters investigation, and that charges against principals at other companies that specialize in ransomware negotiation may be forthcoming.</p> <p>Immediately after Van der Stap&#8217;s arrest, another member of ShinyHunters named &#8220;<strong>Rey</strong>&#8221; assumed control over the group and began taunting the FBI over data the group stole from the agency&#8217;s online recruitment portal, which included each’s person’s unit and specialization, as well as medical and psychiatric records.</p> <p>Last week, Reuters reported that Rey &#8212; identified as a teenager named <strong>Saif Al-din Khader</strong> &#8212; had been detained and was cooperating with FBI investigators. On October 7, we <a href="https://krebsonsecurity.com/2026/10/shinyhunters-extorted-boeing-spin-off-prior-to-arrests/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">detailed</a> how Rey was apprehended as the cybercrime group allegedly sought to extort a navigation and digital aviation unit that was divested by Boeing in late 2025.</p> <p><em>This is likely to be a fast-moving story. Updates will be noted along with timestamps.</em></p>
